Official driving theory exam question pool - 1802
Source: data.gov.il

Question 1. Who is under obligation to be familiar with the traffic laws and obey them?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. Only a pedestrian
   B. Every road user.
   C. Only a professional driver.
   D. Only a driver.

Question 2. Under what conditions can a person drive a motor vehicle?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. The driver must have a valid license for the same class of vehicle.
   B. According to the conditions, laid down in the annual test.
   C. The driver should be the registered owner or have legal control.
   D. According to the conditions laid down in the Manufacturer’s manual.

Question 3. What is the duty of a person who owns or has control of a vehicle?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. Not to permit to anyone to drive, who doesn’t have a valid license for the type of vehicle.
   B. To register any person who drives his vehicle in the vehicle travel log
   C. To allow any person who asks him to drive his vehicle.
   D. To make sure that photocopies of the driver’s license and insurance policy only are carried in the vehicle

Question 4. Apart from a driving instructor, who may escort a “new driver”?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, В]
   A. Any holder of a valid driving license for the same class of vehicle, and aged at least 24 yrs old.
   B. A holder of a driving license for the same class of vehicle for at least 3 years and aged at least 30 yrs old, or a holder of a license for at least 2 yrs and aged at least 35 yrs old.
   C. Any person who has not been convicted for any traffic offence during the past two years.
   D. A holder of a driving license for the same class of vehicle for at least 5 years and aged at least 24 yrs old, or a holder of a license for at least 3 yrs and aged at least 30 yrs old.

Question 5. Is a policeman allowed to detain without a warrant a driver who committed an offence in front of him?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. Not under any condition
   B. Yes, but only under a court certificate
   C. Yes, if the driver doesn’t give his name and address or doesn’t present his license as required
   D. Yes, only if the driver committed an offence that actually endangered road users

Question 6. A police officer may revoke a driver’s license for a period of thirty days:
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. When the driver endangers the life of a person on a pedestrian crossing
   B. An arrest warrant was issued against the vehicle’s owner
   C. When the driver committed an offence of holding a driver’s license that has been expired for more than ten days
   D. When the passenger on the driver’s side did not wear a seatbelt

Question 7. A police officer may revoke a driver’s license for a period of sixty days:
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. When the driver commits a traffic offence which doesn’t cause a road accident
   B. When a driver involved in an accident is reasonably suspected of being responsible for causing injury or causing damage to property.
   C. When the driver is caught speeding
   D. When the driver commits a traffic offence in which a person is killed

Question 8. When may a police officer revoke a driver’s license for a period of ninety days?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. When the driver is involved in a car accident in which only property damage was caused
   B. When the driver disobeys a stop sign
   C. When a driver involved in an accident is reasonably suspected of causing the death of a person.
   D. When the driver fails to give right of way.

Question 9. What is the penalty for driving whilst disqualified?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. A fine of 80,000 NIS
   B. 6 yrs in jail and 2 yrs on probation.
   C. None, if the driver presents a valid driver’s license within 7 days
   D. Up to 3 years in jail, plus other penalties.

Question 10. What is the legal definition of a “road”
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. Any place that is open for public passage, except for city streets
   B. Asphalt or concrete paved road ways only.
   C. The term “road” has no legal definition
   D. Any rail, street, alley, square, passage, bridge and any other open space in which the public has a right to pass through

Question 11. What is the legal definition of “lighting up time”
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. The time period of natural lighting conditions, when there is no need to have the vehicle lights turned on
   B. The night time or any other time of the day in which visibility conditions are poor due to weather conditions.
   C. The time period in which the vehicle’s lights must be turned on while driving in a freeway
   D. The night time period, between sunset and sunrise

Question 12. Define “parking”
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
Image: https://www.gov.il/BlobFolder/generalpage/tq_pic_01/he/TQ_PIC_3012.jpg
   A. Stopping a vehicle for a defined period of time, in order to unload cargo, intermittently
   B. Stopping the vehicle for more than thirty seconds
   C. Standing a vehicle in order to load it with cargo only, intermittently
   D. Standing a vehicle for an uninterrupted period of time, not for the purpose of immediate loading or unloading of passengers or loading or unloading of goods.

Question 13. A utility tractor is a tractor type that:
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, 1, В]
   A. Has at least two wheels, an all up weight of not more than 1,500 kg, and is steered with a handlebar
   B. Has four wheels, an unlimited all up weight, and is steered with a handlebar
   C. Has at least three wheels and an all up weight of more than 12,000 kg
   D. Has at least four wheels, an all up weight of not more than 1,500 kg, and is steered with a wheel

Question 14. Define “ATV (All Terrain Vehicle)”:
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, 1, В]
   A. A vehicle that has at least four wheels, with the rider sitting astride the vehicle and steering with handlebars.
   B. A vehicle that has at least four wheels and is steered with a wheel.
   C. A vehicle that has at least two wheels, with the rider sitting astride the vehicle and steering with handlebars.
   D. A vehicle that has at least four wheels and is steered with a wheel. The driver sits on it with both legs on one side of the seat.

Question 15. When a no stopping sign (433) is placed on the right side of a two-way highway:
[Topic: Traffic signs | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
Image: https://www.gov.il/BlobFolder/generalpage/tq_pic_01/he/TQ_PIC_3015.jpg
   A. Stopping is prohibited on both sides of the vehicle.
   B. Stopping is prohibited on the right side of the roadway and on the hard shoulder.
   C. Stopping is prohibited on the roadway only.
   D. Stopping is prohibited on the hard shoulder only.

Question 16. Define “one way street”
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. Any path in which vehicle traffic is allowed in one direction only
   B. Any roadway in which vehicle traffic is allowed in one direction only
   C. Any roadway in which only motor vehicle traffic is allowed in one direction only
   D. Any roadway in which vehicle traffic is allowed in both directions

Question 17. What is “night time”?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. The period of time in which it is required to turn on the vehicle’s parking lights
   B. A period of heavy fog only
   C. The period of time that starts 15 minutes following sunset and ends 15 minutes before sunrise
   D. The period of time between sunset and the following sunrise.

Question 18. The following picture shows a pavement. Which of the following sentences is correct?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
Image: https://www.gov.il/BlobFolder/generalpage/tq_pic_01/he/TQ_PIC_3018.jpg
   A. Motorcycles are permitted to use the pavement instead of the road.
   B. The pavement is designed for pedestrians only.
   C. Private passenger vehicles are always permitted to park temporarily on the pavement, when there is no parking space on the road.
   D. Bicyclists are permitted to ride on the pavement as well.

Question 19. What is an “Interchange”?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. The area that is formed by the meeting of at least three roadways, at the same level.
   B. A meeting of at least two roadways at the same level in a freeway.
   C. A meeting point of roads at different levels, in which it is possible to drive and pass from one road to the other
   D. A junction of roads at the same level in an urban area.

Question 20. “Permitted load” on a commercial vehicle is:
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C]
   A. The maximum weight of the cargo (in Kg) that is permitted to be loaded on the vehicle
   B. The weight of the cargo plus the vehicle’s own weight
   C. The all up weight of a transport vehicle
   D. The vehicle’s own weight, including: water, oil, fuel and the weight of the driver

Question 21. The boundary of the intersection / junction is determined by:
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. The stop lines on each of the roads that meet in the intersection
   B. The place in which traffic signs are posted before the intersection
   C. The imaginary line drawn between the pavements or kerbs of each road meeting the junction.
   D. The meeting of two or more pedestrian crossings in the intersection

Question 22. A back seat passenger must wear a seatbelt. Who is held responsible if a back seat passenger doesn’t wear his seat-belt?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, В]
   A. The driver only
   B. The driver and the passenger
   C. The passenger only
   D. The owner of the vehicle

Question 23. What is a “pedestrian crossing”?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. A part of the pavement that is marked for pedestrian crossing
   B. A part of the road designated and marked for pedestrians to cross.
   C. A part of the road edge that is marked for pedestrian crossing
   D. A part of the road that is marked for pedestrian crossing, including the pavement

Question 24. Define level crossing:
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. A place where a road and a railroad meet on a freeway.
   B. A place where a road and a railroad meet at different levels.
   C. A place where a road and a railroad meet or cross at the same level.
   D. Any place where a railroad can be crossed

Question 25. What is “all up weight”?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C]
   A. A vehicle’s own weight, as approved by the Licensing Authority
   B. A vehicle’s own weight, plus the weight of the people and cargo that the vehicle is allowed to drive or carry, as approved by the Licensing Authority
   C. A vehicle’s own volume plus the weight of the people and cargo it is allowed to drive or carry, as approved by the Licensing Authority
   D. The volume of the vehicle and of the cargo it is allowed to drive or carry, as approved by the Licensing Authority

Question 26. What is “own weight”?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, 1]
   A. The vehicle’s weight with cargo but without the driver.
   B. The vehicle’s weight without cargo and without the driver.
   C. The vehicle’s weight with cargo and with a driver
   D. The vehicle’s volume without cargo but without the driver

Question 27. When a driver gives “right of way”, it means that:
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. He merges with traffic without causing an accident
   B. He allows other drivers to continue driving without causing them to change their speed or to stop
   C. He barely manages to push into the traffic flow
   D. He enters the traffic flow in the same speed as the other vehicles.

Question 28. When is it permitted to overtake, in one go, two vehicle that are driving before you on your lane
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. Only on highways.
   B. When overtaking is not prohibited, and when the overtaking can be safely completed.
   C. This type of overtaking is absolutely prohibited by law.
   D. Only when overtaking two private passenger cars.

Question 29. Define an “axle” of a vehicle:
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D]
   A. The vehicle’s back beam to which the vehicle’s spare wheel is connected.
   B. A shaft which transmits the rotary motion from the gearbox to the engine.
   C. Shaft or system of shafts running the width of the vehicle attached to the wheels.
   D. The vehicle’s front part, designed to absorb the impact of low-speed collisions

Question 30. Who is a “road user”?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. A person who uses the road for standing or running only
   B. A person who uses the road for riding a motor vehicle only
   C. A person who uses the road for bicycle riding only
   D. A person who uses the road for driving, traveling, riding, walking, standing or any other purpose

Question 31. Define “to dip the lights”:
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. Turning off the high beam and turning on the fog lights
   B. Changing the height of the parking beam
   C. Reverting from low beam to high beam.
   D. Reverting from high beam to low beam.

Question 32. Define “Junction”:
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. A meeting point of two roads or more.
   B. The area formed by the junction of two or more roads at different levels.
   C. The area formed by the junction of a railroad and a roadway
   D. The junction of two urban roadways, provided that they are both asphalt-paved.

Question 33. Define “stop line”:
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. A line drawn on either full or half width across the road marking a stopping point for vehicles.
   B. A line along the road, marking the point in which a vehicle must stop, in junctions with traffic lights only.
   C. A line along the road, marking the point in which a vehicle must stop, before a railway crossing
   D. A junction-line along the road, either full or partial, marking the point in which a vehicle must stop near a pavement

Question 34. Define “play street”:
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]

   B. A road designed for children’s games, pedestrians and vehicles, sign posted at the entrance with the “play street” sign.
   C. A street designated for children’s games, into which only pedestrians and two wheeled vehicles are permitted to enter.
   D. A street in which different types of vehicles may drive side by side

Question 35. What is a “rigid veicle”?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C]
   A. A vehicle body which exceeds the size of a normal vehicle.
   B. A vehicle body in which the driver’s compartment is separated from the trunk.
   C. A vehicle body with a partition between the driver’s seat and the passengers.
   D. A vehicle body in which the driver’s compartment and the trunk comprise one unit.

Question 36. Define “slow moving vehicle”:
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: 1, В]
   A. A motor vehicle having a prescribed maximal speed of 50 k.p.h.
   B. A motor vehicle having a prescribed maximal speed of no less than 50 k.p.h.
   C. A vehicle restricted by law to 40 k.p.h. or less
   D. A motor vehicle having a prescribed maximal speed of 55 k.p.h.

Question 37. Which of the following is an emergency vehicle?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. A Red Magen David Ambulance, an Israel Police vehicle, IDF vehicle, Fire-fighting vehicle, integrated policing vehicle or any other vehicle approved by the Authority, when it sounds its emergency signals and operates flashing lights and sounds an alarm signal.
   B. Any ambulance, IDF vehicle, fire-fighting vehicle or other commercial vehicle that was authorized to function as an emergency vehicle by the Licensing Authority
   C. Every vehicle of the Israel Police or the IDF and vehicles that are used for regular security missions in borderline settlements
   D. A vehicle of the Israel Police or the IDF and a fire-fighting vehicle only

   D. No, except with a permission of the Tax Authorities

   D. If the street is not lit, you should turn on the parking lights.

[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]

   D. Any part of the road (pavement, hard shoulder, traffic island) which is elevated above the road way.

Question 50. When may a driver be in possession of only photocopies of his original vehicle documents?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. Only a vehicle transport company is allowed to hold a photocopy of the vehicle registration
   B. It is only permitted when the vehicle is owned by a corporation, and the photocopied documents’ validity is authorized by the safety officer’s signature and stamp on them.
   C. When the vehicle is owned by a private person, and the photocopy is certified by the vehicle owner’s signature
   D. Never. The driver must always be in possession of the original documents

   B. No, a certified photocopy is sufficient
   C. No, a photocopy signed by the vehicle’s owner is sufficient
   D. Yes, at the time determined by the vehicle’s driver

[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]

   B. It is permitted only to add or change an address, in the main post office branch
   C. It is permitted only to add the zip code of a residence address, in the main post office branch

Question 57. Which changes in health condition must be reported to the Licensing Authority?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. The driver doesn’t have to report anything to the Licensing Authority, but only to the Ministry of Health
   B. Any detection of the following conditions: heart disease or any limitation of the nervous system, bones, eye-sight and hearing
   C. The driver doesn’t have to report anything to the Licensing Authority. The report shall be made by the treating physician of the driver
   D. A driver doesn’t have to report about changes in his health, except in case of a neurological problem

Question 58. Should every road user obey the traffic signs?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. Yes, while on the sidewalk only.
   B. Yes, every road user should obey the road signs.
   C. No, only vehicle drivers should obey the traffic signs
   D. Yes, while on the road way only.

Question 59. Do traffic light instructions have priority over “right-of-way giving” signs?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. No, In legal terms, the instructions of a traffic light and the instructions of traffic signs are of equal effect
   B. No, but a stop sign has priority over a green light for traffic moving in the same direction
   C. Yes, traffic light instructions have priority over any type of traffic sign, except for a flashing yellow light
   D. Sometimes. Traffic light instructions have priority in an urban way only

Question 60. Is it obligatory to obey the directions and signals of a policeman in uniform?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. Yes, always, while taking extra precautions
   B. No, only to the directions of a traffic policeman
   C. No, only if the policeman is on duty
   D. Yes, only as regards to traffic directions

Question 61. Is it obligatory to obey the directions of a policeman who has identified himself with an appropriate certificate?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. No. you must only obey a traffic policeman who directs traffic in an intersection.
   B. Yes, even if his directions contradict those of a road sign.
   C. Generally not.
   D. Yes, but only concerning traffic directions that are given within the intersection.

Question 62. Is it obligatory to obey the directions and signals of a municipal inspector in uniform?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. No. It is only obligatory to obey as regards to parking directions within city limits
   B. No. It is only obligatory to obey as regards to the presentation of a driver’s license
   C. Usually not
   D. Yes, if the directions are related to traffic control.

Question 63. Is it obligatory to obey the directions of a Public Works Department employee in uniform?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. No, unless he directs you to stop on the road’s “hard shoulders” only.
   B. Yes, but on urban roads only.
   C. Yes, wherever road pavement or maintenance works are conducted.
   D. No, it is only obligatory to obey a policeman in uniform,

Question 64. Is it obligatory to obey the directions and signals of a military policeman in uniform?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. No, only for the purpose of stopping a vehicle
   B. Yes, equally to a civil policeman
   C. No, only as regards to the presentation of a driver’s license
   D. Usually not

Question 65. Is it obligatory to obey the directions and signals of a policeman, even when a certain direction or signal is opposed to the traffic signs?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. Yes, except for directions which contradict a stop sign.
   B. Yes, it is obligatory to obey with full caution.
   C. No, it is not obligatory to obey
   D. Yes, except for directions which contradict a no-entry sign.

Question 66. Is it obligatory to obey the directions and signals of a military policeman, even when a certain direction or signal is opposed to the traffic signs?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. Yes, it is obligatory to obey with full caution.
   B. Yes, except for directions which contradict a no-entry sign.
   C. No, it is not obligatory to obey
   D. Yes, except for directions which contradict a stop sign.

Question 67. Is it obligatory to obey the directions and signals of an authorized municipal inspector, even when a certain direction or signal is opposed to the traffic signs?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C, D, A, 1, В]
   A. Yes, except in the case of a “stop” sign

   D. It is prohibited to drive faster than 60 k.p.h during the night. During the day, however, it is permitted.

   C. Taking account of road and traffic conditions, in a manner that the driver maintains absolute control of the vehicle and can stop safely at all times.
   D. When the driving speed is determined at the driver’s own discretion, and provided that it doesn’t exceed the statutory speed limit.

   C. No, the weight indicated in the bill of lading is not a final, solid and accepted fact.

Question 1409. Is it obligatory to mark oversize freight on a vehicle?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C]
   A. No. A vehicle with oversize freight should only be marked if the freight sticks out from the vehicle’s rear side.
   B. Yes, it is obligatory to mark the oversize freight, but only during “light time”.
   C. No. When a vehicle is standing on the road, its oversize freight doesn’t cause any risk; hence, it is not obligatory to mark it.
   D. Yes, it is always obligatory to mark oversize freight.

   B. Yes, but it is only obligatory to mark a vehicle with an oversize weight on its front side.
   C. No, It is obligatory to mark oversize freight from the front and from the rear of the vehicle, but only if the freight exceeds the vehicle by 150 cm.
   D. Yes, it is always obligatory to mark oversize freight.

Question 1411. How should oversize freight be marked from the vehicle’s front or rear sides?

   C. The freight shall be marked from its front with a red triangle, and from its rear with a white triangle.
   D. The freight shall be marked from its front with white light reflecting triangle, and from its rear with a white triangle with red light-reflecting margins.

   C. Yes, it is permitted to transport freight containers in every vehicle equipped with cables to safely attach the container to the vehicle.
   D. Yes, containers can be transported in every vehicle that has a high front wall.

Question 1440. When is a driver required to drive on the “hard shoulder” - where the “shoulder” is asphalt-paved and free of traffic - in order to allow another vehicle to overtake him?
[Topic: Traffic laws | Licenses: C1, C]
   A. When the vehicle drives very slowely for any reason.
   B. Only when the vehicle is forced to drive slowly on a steep uphill climb, and disturbs the free traffic.
   C. When a driver of a motor vehicle is forced to drive very slowly due to the freight he’s carrying or to the steepness of the road, and he delays the traffic behind him.
   D. Never. It is prohibited to drive on the “hard shoulders”; hence, a vehicle that delays the traffic behind it should pull into the “hard shoulder” and stop, to allow the traffic congestion behind him to open up.

Question 1708. The one of the functions of the grooves in the tyre tread
[Topic: Know your vehicle | Licenses: C1, A, 1, В]
